- `Narrowing.narrow_nil(type)` — the nil fragment of `type`. `Constant[nil]` and `Nominal[NilClass]` are preserved; non-nil `Constant`/`Nominal` carriers collapse to `Bot`; `Union` recurses element-wise; `Top`/`Dynamic[T]` MUST narrow to the canonical `Constant[nil]` so downstream dispatch resolves through `NilClass`; carriers that never inhabit nil (`Singleton`, `Tuple`, `HashShape`) collapse to `Bot`; `Bot` is its own nil fragment.
- `Narrowing.narrow_non_nil(type)` — the non-nil fragment of `type`. Mirror of `narrow_nil`: nil-only carriers collapse to `Bot` and non-nil carriers are preserved; `Top`/`Dynamic[T]`/`Singleton`/`Tuple`/`HashShape` flow through unchanged; `Union` recurses element-wise.
- `Narrowing.narrow_equal(type, literal)` (Slice 6 phase 2 sub-phase 2) — the equality fragment of `type` against a trusted `Type::Constant` literal. String, Symbol, and Integer literals MUST narrow only when `type` is already a finite trusted literal domain (`Constant` or a `Union` of trusted constants). Nil, true, and false are singleton values and MAY be extracted from mixed domains such as `Integer | nil`, but MUST NOT narrow `Dynamic[top]` into the compared literal. Float literals MUST NOT narrow. Untrusted inputs return `type` unchanged.
- `Narrowing.narrow_not_equal(type, literal)` (Slice 6 phase 2 sub-phase 2) — the domain-relative complement of `narrow_equal`. It removes the compared literal from finite trusted literal domains and removes nil/true/false singleton members from mixed domains when present. It MUST NOT create an unbounded difference type for broad domains such as `String` or `Dynamic[top]`.
- `Narrowing.narrow_class(type, class_name, exact: false, environment: Environment.default, scope: nil)` (Slice 6 phase 2 sub-phase 1) — the class-membership fragment of `type`. The `class_name` argument is the qualified name of the asked class as it appears in source (`"Integer"`, `"Foo::Bar"`); `exact: false` models `is_a?`/`kind_of?` (subclass-inclusive) and `exact: true` models `instance_of?` (exact). The carrier rules MUST be:
  - `Constant[v]` — preserved when `v.class` satisfies the predicate (subclass-or-equal of the asked class under `exact: false`; equal under `exact: true`). It MUST collapse to `Bot` only on a KNOWN-disjoint ordering: `Bot` is the assertion "this can never match", and an `:unknown` verdict is the environment declining to decide rather than evidence for it. Under `exact: false` an `:unknown` ordering MUST yield `Dynamic[top]` — the same join `Nominal` takes, and for the same reason (keeping the old bound licenses `call.undefined-method` on the branch the guard just proved). An in-source `include` into a core class never reaches the ordering, so the collapse reported `case 1 when Taggable` unreachable on an arm MRI takes ([#657](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/657)). `exact: true` MUST still collapse, because `instance_of?` compares the exact class and a module is never an object's class.
  - `Nominal[C]` — when the bound `C` is the same as the asked class (or already its subclass under `exact: false`) it is preserved. When the asked class is a subclass of `C` under `exact: false` the type narrows DOWN to `Nominal[asked]` (e.g., `Nominal[Numeric]` under `is_a?(Integer)` becomes `Nominal[Integer]`). Disjoint hierarchies under `exact: false` and any non-equal class under `exact: true` collapse to `Bot`. When either class name does not resolve through the supplied analyzer `Environment`, the result MUST stay conservative and preserve the input type.
  - `Union` — recurses element-wise, dropping disjoint members.
  - `Tuple[*]`/`HashShape{*}` — projected uniformly through `"Array"`/`"Hash"` against the asked class, under the same `:unknown` decline as `Constant`: a project that reopens `Array` or `Hash` to include a module leaves that ordering undecidable exactly as `Integer` does.
  - `Singleton[C]` — projected uniformly through `"Class"` against the asked class (the inhabitants are class objects), under the same `:unknown` decline. The projection is silent about `extend`, which puts a module in the class object's SINGLETON ancestry and so makes `Widget.is_a?(Comparable)` true where the `Class` projection answers `:disjoint`; a `Bot` under `exact: false` MUST therefore additionally be declined (yielding `Dynamic[top]`) when either singleton-ancestry record for `C` holds a module ordering `:equal`, `:subclass` or `:unknown` against the asked class ([#898](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/898), [#915](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/915)). The two records are read as one union: the supplied `scope`'s source-side `extend` record for `C` — its own, or one inherited up the as-written superclass chain — and the environment's declaration-side answer, the MODULE names RBS puts in `C`'s singleton ancestry (`Environment#singleton_extended_modules`), which is how an `extend` declared only in a project's own `sig/` reaches this edge. `:superclass` and `:disjoint` verdicts support nothing and MUST keep the collapse, so `case Widget when Integer` still reports on a `Widget` that extends `Comparable`. The record is evidence FOR a singleton ancestor only: it MUST NOT be read on the negative edge and MUST NOT preserve `Singleton[C]` on the positive one, because the walk that builds it cannot observe a runtime `extend` and so can never establish absence. A nil `scope` (the entries that carry an environment alone) reads as an empty source-side record, and a class the environment does not know reads as an empty declaration-side one.
  - `Narrowing.narrow_not_class` takes the same optional `scope` for signature symmetry and MUST ignore it.
  - `Top`/`Dynamic[T]` — narrow to `Nominal[asked]` so downstream dispatch can resolve through the asked class.
  - `Bot` — preserved.
- `Narrowing.narrow_not_class(type, class_name, exact: false, environment: Environment.default)` (Slice 6 phase 2 sub-phase 1) — the falsey fragment of the same predicate. `Constant`/`Nominal`/`Union`/`Tuple`/`HashShape`/`Singleton` carriers that DO satisfy the predicate collapse to `Bot`; carriers that do NOT satisfy it are preserved unchanged (for `Nominal` this means `Nominal[Numeric]` under `!is_a?(Integer)` stays `Nominal[Numeric]`, since the analyzer cannot prove the disjunction without a richer carrier). `Top`/`Dynamic[T]`/`Bot` flow through unchanged.

These primitives MUST be deterministic and structurally pure: two calls with structurally-equal inputs produce structurally-equal outputs, and calling them never alters the input.

### Predicate-level narrowing

`Narrowing.predicate_scopes(node, scope)` MUST return an `[truthy_scope, falsey_scope]` pair. When `node` is `nil` or its shape is not in the recognised catalogue the pair MUST be `[scope, scope]` so the caller observes "no narrowing" without a special return value. The recognised Slice 6 phase 1 catalogue is:

- `Prism::ParenthesesNode` — recurse into the body when present.
- `Prism::StatementsNode` — analyse the last statement; earlier statements MAY have scope effects, but the StatementEvaluator has already produced the post-predicate scope before calling `Narrowing`, so the analyser does NOT thread additional effects.
- `Prism::LocalVariableReadNode` — when the local is bound in `scope`, narrow truthy → `narrow_truthy(local)` and falsey → `narrow_falsey(local)`. Unbound locals fall through to the no-narrowing fallback.
- `Prism::CallNode` — the catalogue covers six predicate shapes, all rejected silently when the call has a block or its receiver is `nil`:
  - `recv.nil?` (no positional/keyword argument): narrow the receiver-local through `narrow_nil`/`narrow_non_nil`.
  - unary `!recv` (`name == :!`, no positional/keyword argument): analyse `recv` and swap the resulting pair.
  - `recv.is_a?(C)` / `recv.kind_of?(C)` / `recv.instance_of?(C)` (Slice 6 phase 2 sub-phase 1): require the receiver to be a `Prism::LocalVariableReadNode` and the single argument to be a static constant reference (`Prism::ConstantReadNode` or `Prism::ConstantPathNode`). The qualified name MUST be rendered via the parent-walk `Foo::Bar` form. The truthy edge rebinds the local through `narrow_class(local, class_name, exact:, environment: scope.environment)` (`exact: true` only for `instance_of?`); the falsey edge rebinds through `narrow_not_class(local, class_name, exact:, environment: scope.environment)`. Anything else (a local-only argument, a method-call argument, multiple arguments) MUST fall through to the no-narrowing branch.
  - `local == literal` / `literal == local` and the `!=` mirror (Slice 6 phase 2 sub-phase 2): require one side to be a `Prism::LocalVariableReadNode` and the other side to be a static trusted literal (`String`, `Symbol`, `Integer`, `true`, `false`, or `nil`; not `Float`). The truthy edge of `==` rebinds the local through `narrow_equal`; the falsey edge rebinds through `narrow_not_equal`. `!=` swaps those edges. Each recognised equality predicate MUST also attach a `FactStore::Fact`: use the `local_binding` bucket when the local type changed, otherwise the `relational` bucket so broad or dynamic domains retain the relation without unsound positive narrowing.
  - `recv.empty?` / `recv.any?` / `recv.none?` (no block, no argument) on an `Array`-typed receiver-local (ADR-47 §4-4): narrow to `non-empty-array[T]` on the edge that implies at least one element — the **false** edge of `empty?`, the **true** edge of `any?`, the **false** edge of `none?`. The opposite edge is the conservative no-op.
  - `local < lit` / `local <= lit` / `local > lit` / `local >= lit` (and the literal-on-the-left mirror, transposed), `local.between?(lit, lit)`, and the zero-class predicates `positive?` / `negative?` / `zero?` / `nonzero?`, where `lit` is a `Prism::IntegerNode` or `Prism::FloatNode`: the Integer-rooted members of the local narrow on both edges through `narrow_integer_comparison` (an Integer literal only); the Float-rooted members narrow on the **truthy edge only** through `narrow_float_comparison` (`Float[c..]`, `Float[...c]`, `Float[..c]`; ADR-109 WD5) and keep the entry type on the falsy edge, which admits `NaN`. `between?` narrows its truthy edge only for both classes; the zero-class predicates model `Integer` only.
  - `local.nan?` / `local.finite?` (no argument): `nan?` narrows the **falsey** edge of the Float-rooted members to `non-nan-float` and leaves the truthy edge alone; `finite?` narrows the **truthy** edge to `finite-float` and leaves the falsey edge alone. Non-Float members are untouched on both edges.
  - `recv.respond_to?(sym)` with a single statically-known `Symbol`/`String` argument (template-corpora survey): the truthy edge removes `nil` from the receiver-local through `narrow_non_nil` (a truthy `respond_to?` proves the receiver was not `nil`), UNLESS `sym` is a method `NilClass` itself defines (`:to_s`, `:inspect`, `:nil?`, …), in which case it declines. The falsey edge is always the no-op, and narrowing never promotes a non-nil type.
  - `subject =~ pattern` / `pattern =~ subject` (regex match-data globals; the binding contract is [`control-flow-analysis.md`](../type-specification/control-flow-analysis.md#regexp-match-predicate-narrowing)): exactly one operand MUST resolve to a regex pattern — a syntactic `Prism::RegularExpressionNode`, or a `Prism::ConstantReadNode`/`Prism::ConstantPathNode` whose type resolves (through `Reflection.resolve_constant_type`, the shared lexical-constant owner) to a value-pinned `Type::Constant` wrapping a `Regexp`. The analyser MUST decline (both edges = the entry scope) when neither operand resolves to a regex, when both do, when a constant operand types as a `Type::Union`, or when the resolved pattern is extended-mode (`RegularExpressionNode#extended?` / `Regexp#options & Regexp::EXTENDED`) — the light char-scan participation walker miscounts a comment `(` as a capture group, so its indices are untrustworthy. On the truthy edge `$~` binds to `Nominal[MatchData]`, `$&`/`` $` ``/`$'` to `Nominal[String]`, and each *unconditional* numbered group `$N` (per `unconditional_capture_groups`: no zero-permitting quantifier `?`/`*`/`{0,…}` on the group or an ancestor, no enclosing alternation; `(?:…)`/lookaround do not capture) to `Nominal[String]`; on the falsey edge those same globals bind to `Constant[nil]`. `$+` (the last matched group) is gated on the participation set exactly as `$N` is: it binds only when at least one group is unconditional, and otherwise stays unbound (RBS `String?`) on both edges, because a zero-group or all-optional-group pattern leaves `$+` nil even on a successful match. Conditional groups' `$N` are left unbound on both edges. A `!~` predicate binds neither edge yet ([#1379](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1379)); it only invalidates, as below. The globals are frame-local facts ([#1358](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1358)): Ruby keeps them in the special-variable slot of the method, class / module or file body that runs the match, every block and closure created in that body shares the slot, and a called method or a nested `def` / class / module body has its own. `StatementEvaluator#rebinds_match_globals?` forgets them (`Scope#forget_match_globals`) after a statement-position call that may rebind the slot: a call `MatchRebinding::Calls.statement_rebinds?` counts (`#match_capable_call?`, which reads the call's operands where they were typed, `#operand_scope`). The reading is split by what the analyzer did before [#1365](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1365), so that no position newly keeps a narrowing Ruby rebinds and none newly forgets without evidence. A call `MatchRebinding::Calls.base_named?` names (a `MatchRebinding::MATCH_CAPABLE_METHODS` name on any receiver, or for an implicit-self / `self.` call a `SelfCalls.named_match?` name) forgets unless `Calls.forgets_by_name?` proves it cannot match: `match?`; `grep` / `grep_v` without a block; a `LOOKUPS` name (`[]`, `slice`, `slice!`, `index`, `rindex`, `byteindex`, `byterindex`, `partition`, `rpartition`, `split`, `start_with?`, `any?`, `all?`, `none?`, `one?`), a block-form `grep` / `grep_v`, or `[]=` (its index arguments) whose arguments all satisfy `Operands.keep_literal?` (a `Prism::SymbolNode`, `Prism::StringNode`, number, `nil` / `true` / `false` node, or a `Prism::RangeNode` over those), except a `split` with no argument or a `Prism::NilNode` first one, which splits on `$;` and keeps only while the file's program-global census (`Scope#program_globals`, which records plain `Prism::GlobalVariableWriteNode`s only, not operator writes or multiple-assignment targets) holds no `$;` / `$-F` write; and `===` whose receiver is such a literal or a constant that types as a `Type::Singleton` (`Operands.class_constant?`). An inferred argument type proves nothing there, because a flow type can be stale while the value is a Regexp ([#1380](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1380)); `=~`, `!~`, `match`, `sub`, `gsub`, `scan`, `~`, an eval and a `send` always forget. Any other call forgets only when `Calls.rebinds?` shows it matches: an `ALWAYS_MATCHING` name (`=~`, `!~`, `match`, `sub`, `sub!`, `gsub`, `gsub!`, `scan`); a `LOOKUPS` name with an argument `Operands.known_regexp_operand?` accepts, and `grep` / `grep_v` on the same terms with a block; `[]=` with such an index argument; `===` or `~` on such a receiver; a `split` with no argument or a `nil` first one when the census types `$;` / `$-F` as a Regexp (`Operands.regexp_type?`); `eval`, `instance_eval`, `class_eval` or `module_eval` whose first argument's code may match (`Calls.code_reading`: a `Prism::StringNode` whose `unescaped` code `Prism.parse`s and whose program `MatchRebinding.program_may_match?` accepts — the block scan; a `Prism::StringNode` that does not parse does not match; a `Prism::InterpolatedStringNode` read the same way with each interpolation replaced by an identifier; the answer is kept on the frame under `Frame#memo`'s `:code` kind), and an `eval` whose code the analyzer cannot read when its receiver is a call named `binding` or the `Kernel` constant. Code it cannot read is any other argument node, an interpolated String that does not parse once so replaced, code over 64 KB or with brackets nested over 256 deep (not parsed), and code whose parse or scan raises (`SystemStackError` included); `instance_eval` / `class_eval` / `module_eval` of such code does not count; and a `send` / `__send__` / `public_send` whose first argument is a `Prism::SymbolNode` / `Prism::StringNode` whose `unescaped` bytes name (`Calls::NAMES_BY_STRING`, compared as Strings, so an invalid byte cannot raise) a method `rebinds?` counts with the remaining arguments, or a computed name sent an argument `known_regexp_operand?` accepts, unless the name is an interpolated String or Symbol whose last part ends in `=` (an attribute writer; not `==`, `!=`, `<=`, `>=`). An index `||=` / `&&=` / `op=` node counts on its index arguments the same way. `known_regexp_operand?` accepts a regex literal, `Regexp.new` / `.union` / `.compile`, a splat whose `Type::Tuple` elements or `Array` type argument is a Regexp, and a node whose `Scope#type_of` is one (`Operands.regexp_type?`): a `Type::Constant` wrapping a `Regexp`, a `Type::Nominal` named `Regexp` or that `Environment#class_ordering` places `:subclass` / `:equal` to it, a union or intersection with such a member, a `Type::Difference` / `Type::Refined` over one, or a `Type::Dynamic` whose static facet is one; `Dynamic[top]` and a non-Regexp literal are not. `MatchRebinding::Calls.statement_rebinds?` also counts an implicit-self / `self.` call that may reach the slot although its method does not match; a called Ruby-defined method has a slot of its own, so any other implicit-self call is match-free ([#1364](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1364)). The implicit-self call counts where `MatchRebinding::Frame#self_call_fallback?` holds or no body stamped a frame, as every implicit-self call did before, and when `MatchRebinding.operand_may_match?` finds, in its arguments outside a block or lambda, a call `Calls.base_named?` names on any receiver that `Calls.forgets_by_name?` does not prove match-free, a literal `SelfCalls.method_name_literal?` accepts, a `Prism::YieldNode`, or any other construct the block scan counts under the broad reading. `MatchRebinding.self_call_fallback?` finds in the body or parameters a `Prism::BlockNode` or `Prism::LambdaNode` whose body `MatchRebinding.broad_may_match?` accepts (not a root block itself, below, whose parameters and body it still reads), a call named `binding` on any receiver or a `SelfCalls.sends_binding?` send, or a forward of the method's own block (a `Prism::BlockArgumentNode` that is anonymous or reads the `&block` parameter's name, or a `Prism::ForwardingArgumentsNode`); `Frame` keeps the answer while the scope's local and instance-variable tables are the same objects, as `Frame#memo` keeps a scan. The broad reading is the block scan under `Operands`' `broad:` reading, which counts a constant that does not resolve (`Type::Dynamic`, as a Regexp from another file reads, [#1373](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1373)) and a splat of one as a possible Regexp in a `when` / `in` value, a `===` receiver and a lookup argument; a `BROAD_ARGUMENT` name with any argument `Operands.pattern_value?(…, broad: true)` accepts; a call `SelfCalls.named_match?` reads by name alone (a `SELF_MATCHING` builtin — `!~`, `~`, `start_with?`, `byteindex`, `byterindex`, `[]=` — whatever its arguments, a `PATTERN_PREDICATES` name — `any?`, `all?`, `none?`, `one?` — with an argument, an `eval`, an `instance_eval` / `class_eval` / `module_eval` with an argument, or a `send` / `__send__` / `public_send` whose first argument is computed or a literal whose bytes are in `SelfCalls::METHOD_NAMES`); a method-name literal; a `Prism::YieldNode`; and a `call` / `yield` / `[]` / `===` on a local named as the method's own `&block`. A statement `yield` and a statement call on the method's own block do not count: they rebind the frame only through a caller's C-function proc, a documented gap. The call's receiver chain and arguments run before its method, so `StatementEvaluator#eval_call` forgets the globals in the scope the call runs from (`#forget_operand_match_globals`, before the call's own block entry) when `MatchRebinding.operands_may_rebind?` finds there, through `MatchRebinding.value_may_rebind?`, a call `Calls.rebinds?` counts (none of them forgot before, so `base_named?` plays no part), an index compound write on the same terms, a `Prism::BlockNode` whose body `may_match?` accepts, a block argument `block_argument_may_match?` accepts, or any other construct `matching_node?` counts under the narrow reading. `MatchRebinding.block_entry` forgets on the same terms when it is given the owning call, and `ExpressionTyper#block_return_type_for` and `#try_receiver_block_folds` type the call's block, its captured-local fixpoint and the per-element, inject and hash-shape folds under a typer whose scope has forgotten them (`#rebound_operand_typer`), so every pass reads `[u.index(/(q)/)].map { $1 }` as `[String?]`; `operands_may_rebind?` keeps its answer on the frame under `Frame#memo`'s `:operands` kind, so the statement pass and each typing pass over the call share one scan. An implicit-self call there is read by `Calls.rebinds?` alone, without the fallback or the frameless default, and a `Prism::LambdaNode`, a `def` / class / module body and a `defined?` operand are not looked into. A handler that types a value without evaluating its calls as statements forgets the globals afterwards on the same `value_may_rebind?` terms (`#forget_rebound_match_globals`): an array, hash, interpolation or range literal (`#eval_value_container`), a `rescue` modifier, a constant write's value, an index or attribute compound write, and a node with no handler (`super`, `yield`, `evaluate`'s default branch). Neither reset runs in an operand evaluator (`in_operand`): the statement that holds the operand answers for every call in it, threaded or not. After the call, `#rebinds_match_globals?` also forgets when the call's own block or block argument may match (`MatchRebinding.block_may_match?`), and at every call in a frame that creates a closure whose body may match (`Scope#match_rebinding_closure?`). That last check reads the `MatchRebinding::Frame` that `Scope#with_match_frame` stamps at a method body's entry, with the method's parameters, whose defaults run in the same frame (`StatementEvaluator#build_method_entry_scope`, `ExpressionTyper#build_user_method_body_scope`), at a class / module body's (`#eval_class_body`) and at the file's (`ScopeIndexer.index`); a join keeps either arm's frame. The closures are a `Prism::LambdaNode` and the block of a `StoredBlockCall` (`lambda`, `proc`, `Proc.new`, `define_method`, …) other than a root block, since invocations are not traced; `matching_closure?` still reads the closures inside a root block. A *root block* ([#1361](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1361)) is the block, a `Prism::BlockNode` or `Prism::BlockArgumentNode`, of a call `Inference::FreshFrameBlocks.root_call?` names: `new`, `start` or `fork` on a `Thread`, or `new` on a `Fiber` or `Ractor`, `Prism::ConstantReadNode` or root `Prism::ConstantPathNode` (`StoredBlockCall.root_constant_name`), which `Scope#type_of` types as the `Type::Singleton` of that core class when a scope is given. It runs with a slot of its own, so every reading of what running code may match — `may_match?`, `broad_may_match?`, `operand_may_match?` and `value_may_rebind?` — skips it (`FreshFrameBlocks.any_frame_child?`), except that a root `Prism::BlockArgumentNode`'s expression is read in its place, since Ruby evaluates it in this frame before the thread or fiber starts; `block_may_match?` answers `value_may_rebind?` of that expression for such a call, and false for a root `Prism::BlockNode`. `self_call_fallback?` (through `any_frame_child?`'s `into_root:`) and `matching_closure?` ask what the code makes rather than what it runs, so they still read the root block's parameters and body: a closure made there may be handed back to the creating thread. "May match" (`MatchRebinding.may_match?`, memoised per node on the frame while the scope's local and instance-variable tables are the same objects, `Frame#memo`) reads a block or closure body on narrower, syntactic terms than `Calls`, which types a statement's operands, anywhere but in a nested `def` / class / module body or a `defined?` operand: a call to `=~` / `!~` / `match` / `sub` / `sub!` / `gsub` / `gsub!` / `scan`; a call to `[]` / `slice` / `slice!` / `index` / `rindex` / `partition` / `rpartition` / `split` / `start_with?` / `byteindex` / `byterindex` / `any?` / `all?` / `none?` / `one?` with an argument known to be a Regexp (`MatchRebinding::Operands.regexp_argument?`: a regex literal, a constant, local or instance variable whose type in the call-site scope is a Regexp, or `Regexp.new` / `.union` / `.compile`; a Regexp arriving as a block parameter or assigned inside the block is unbound in that scope and not counted, a known gap), and `grep` / `grep_v` on the same terms in their block form only; `===` on a receiver that may be a Regexp; a `when` condition of a `Prism::CaseNode` with a predicate, or an `in` / `=>` pattern value outside its `if` / `unless` guard, that may be one (`Operands.pattern_value?` / `.pattern_matches?`: not a non-Regexp literal, nor a constant whose type is not a Regexp — a `Type::Singleton`, `Type::Tuple`, `Type::HashShape`, a non-Regexp constant or nominal, or an unresolved `Dynamic` read as a class — nor a splat of such a constant; a Regexp constant defined in another file currently reads as unresolved, because the #644 census does not publish a cross-file Regexp literal, so it is not counted, [#1373](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1373)); a `Prism::MatchLastLineNode`; a write to `$~`; or a block argument that may be a proc made in this frame (`MatchRebinding.block_argument_may_match?`: not an anonymous `&`, not a `&:name` outside `=~` / `!~` / `match` / `sub` / `sub!` / `gsub` / `gsub!` / `scan` / `===`, and not the method's own `&block` parameter while the body never binds that name, `Frame#forwarded_block?`). `match?` never counts. Every block-entry path — `StatementEvaluator#build_block_entry_scope`, `ExpressionTyper#block_entry_scope`, the per-element fold (`#type_block_body_with_param`) and the captured-local fixpoint (`#converged_captured_bindings`) — enters through `MatchRebinding.block_entry`. Given the owning call, it first enters the block of a call `FreshFrameBlocks.fresh_entry?` names — a root block, or the block of a call named `define_method` / `define_singleton_method` on any receiver — with `FreshFrameBlocks.entry(scope, call)`, whatever the body holds: a root block with the globals forgotten, and a definer block under `Scope#untyped_match_globals`, which rebinds each bound match global to `Dynamic[top]` and leaves an unbound one unbound, since the body reads the definer's slot whenever the method is called (the one place `$_`, [#1359](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1359), and `$!` / `$@`, [#1360](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1360), join once they narrow). Otherwise it forgets the globals when the body may match or the frame creates such a closure, because a later iteration runs after an earlier one rebound them; for a block whose owning call, which `#build_block_entry_scope` and `ExpressionTyper#block_entry_scope` pass, is named `tap` / `then` / `yield_self` (`BlockCallTiming.candidate_name?`), the entry scans the body with `MatchRebinding::ADDED_NAMES` (`!~`, `start_with?`, `byteindex`, `byterindex`, `any?`, `all?`, `none?`, `one?`) left out, as every block was scanned before #1364, because the name cannot show the block runs once (a project `then` may keep it, and a loop runs the call again, [#1375](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1375)); any other body reads the call site's narrowing, including the not-yet-modelled cases the type specification lists ([#1371](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1371), [#1372](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1372)). The scans run only while a match global is bound. A later `Regexp.last_match` fold (`MethodDispatcher::RegexpFolding`) consults the same proven-match bindings rather than re-deriving participation.
- `recv.foo(arg)` with **RBS::Extended predicate annotations** (Slice 7 phase 15): when the receiving method's RBS signature carries `%a{rigor:v1:predicate-if-true <target> is <Class>}` or `%a{rigor:v1:predicate-if-false <target> is <Class>}` annotations on its `RBS::Definition::Method`, the analyser MUST narrow the matching binding on the corresponding edge through `narrow_class`. The parser recognises a strict shape: `<target>` is a Ruby parameter name (matched against the selected overload's `required_positionals` / `optional_positionals`) or `self`; `<Class>` is a single optionally-namespaced class identifier (`String`, `Foo::Bar`, `::Foo`). For parameter targets, narrowing applies only when the matching call argument is a `Prism::LocalVariableReadNode`. For `self` targets, four receiver shapes participate (v0.1.1 Track 1 slice 3): `Prism::LocalVariableReadNode` and `Prism::InstanceVariableReadNode` rebind through `Scope#with_local` / `with_ivar`, and both implicit-self (nil receiver) and `Prism::SelfNode` rebind through `Scope#with_self_type`. The implicit-self path requires `Inference::Narrowing#analyse_call` to allow nil-receiver shapes through to the RBS::Extended path; `resolve_rbs_extended_method` consults `scope.self_type` instead of `scope.type_of(node.receiver)` when the receiver is nil. Other receiver shapes (method chains, expressions) still fall through.
- `recv === local` (Slice 7 phase 4): the case-equality predicate is recognised for three receiver shapes:
  - **Class / Module receiver** (a static `Prism::ConstantReadNode` or `Prism::ConstantPathNode`) — isomorphic to `local.is_a?(receiver)`. The truthy and falsey edges are produced through `class_predicate_scopes`, identical to `is_a?`/`kind_of?`.
  - **Range literal receiver** (`Prism::RangeNode`, optionally wrapped in `Prism::ParenthesesNode`) — narrows `local` on the truthy edge to `Numeric` for integer/float endpoints and to `String` for string endpoints. The falsey edge keeps the entry type because `Range#===` returns false both for non-coercible types and for in-range failures.
  - **Regexp literal receiver** (`Prism::RegularExpressionNode` / `Prism::InterpolatedRegularExpressionNode`) — narrows `local` on the truthy edge to `String`. Same falsey rule as Range.
  Anything else (dynamic receiver, custom `===` method, non-local LHS) MUST fall through to the no-narrowing branch (preserving the entry scope on both edges).
- `Prism::AndNode` — `a && b` narrows the truthy edge with `b`'s truthy scope under `a`'s truthy scope; the falsey edge unions `a`'s falsey scope (b skipped) and `b`'s falsey scope (b ran but returned falsey) via `Scope#join`.
- `Prism::OrNode` — `a || b` narrows the truthy edge with `Scope#join` of `a`'s truthy scope and `b`'s truthy scope (under `a`'s falsey scope); the falsey edge is `b`'s falsey scope under `a`'s falsey scope.
- `Prism::IfNode` / `Prism::UnlessNode` in condition position (issue [#1017](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1017)) — `p ? q : r` (an `unless` swaps its arms; an `elsif` is the nested conditional itself; an absent or empty arm is `nil`) MUST NOT have a combinator of its own. Each arm's edges MUST be taken under the predicate's edge, the `&&` step: `q` under `p`'s truthy scope, `r` under `p`'s falsey scope. The truthy edge MUST be the `Scope#join` of the two arms' truthy scopes, and the falsey edge the join of their falsey scopes, the `||` merge. An arm whose type `Narrowing.predicate_certainty` settles under that edge MUST contribute only its live edge, so a literal `false` / `nil` / `true` arm or an absent arm needs no rule of its own and `p ? false : q` narrows as `!p && q`. The settlement MUST decline when `OptimisticOrigin.resolve` marks the arm optimistic (ADR-101, issue #313). The predicate's own certainty MUST NOT drop an edge: the post-predicate scope has already joined the inner conditional's arms, and a carrier every arm narrowed has lost its optimistic mark there. When the conditionals stacked in the guard, counted through parentheses, a body's last statement, `&&` / `||`, `!`, a predicate and an arm, exceed `Narrowing::CONDITIONAL_GUARD_DEPTH` (2), the guard MUST fall through to the no-narrowing branch; each level analyses the predicate and both arms, and the cap keeps that from multiplying without limit.

The analyser MUST NOT raise on unrecognised predicate shapes, MUST NOT thread any tracer (predicate analysis is a pure query that consults already-typed scope information), and MUST NOT mutate the receiver scope.

### StatementEvaluator integration

`Rigor::Inference::StatementEvaluator` MUST consume the predicate analyser as follows:

- `Prism::IfNode` — after evaluating the predicate to obtain `post_pred`, call `Narrowing.predicate_scopes(node.predicate, post_pred)` to derive `(truthy_scope, falsey_scope)`. The `then` branch MUST be evaluated under `truthy_scope`; the `else` branch (or an absent else, which contributes `Constant[nil]` and the predicate's post-scope) MUST be evaluated under `falsey_scope`. The branch types are unioned and the post-scopes are merged through the existing nil-injection rule.
- `Prism::UnlessNode` — same shape as `IfNode`, but the `then` branch (which runs when the predicate is falsey) MUST be evaluated under `falsey_scope` and the `else` branch under `truthy_scope`.
- **Value position (both nodes).** `ExpressionTyper` MUST type an `IfNode` / `UnlessNode` — the ternary `c ? a : b`, a modifier `a unless c`, or any conditional reached through `Scope#type_of` as an argument, a receiver, a literal element, or a write's value — by delegating to `StatementEvaluator#evaluate` and reading the resulting value, never by typing the arms itself. The narrowing application, the branch elision below, and the terminating-arm rule then have exactly one implementation, so the value of a conditional MUST NOT depend on whether it sits in statement or value position. Issue [#1003](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1003): a second typer that unioned both arms under the entry scope read `f.finite? ? f : 0.0` as `0.0 | Float` while the statement form bound `0.0 | finite-float`.
- **Version-guard precedence (both nodes).** Before the carrier-certainty verdict, `live_branch_for_if`/`#live_branch_for_unless` MUST consult `Rigor::Inference::VersionGuard.verdict(predicate)` — a pure, scope-free function of the AST that decides a version guard from literals the analyzer can read (normative surface: [control-flow-analysis.md § Version-guard condition folding](../type-specification/control-flow-analysis.md#version-guard-condition-folding)). A `:truthy`/`:falsey` verdict MUST elide the dead arm exactly as a `Constant[true]`/`Constant[false]` predicate does, and MUST take precedence over the [ADR-101](../adr/101-optimistic-carrier-branch-elision.md) optimistic-carrier decline, which guards an RBS-derived judgment and has no bearing on a literal one. A `nil` verdict MUST fall through to `Narrowing.predicate_certainty` unchanged. The expression form (`RUBY_VERSION >= "3.1" ? a : b`) reaches the same method through the value-position rule below, so it and the statement form cannot disagree about which arm survives. Because the guard's own expression type stays `bool`, `flow.always-truthy-condition` never sees it — that is the mechanism, not a separate suppression. Eliding the arm silences no diagnostic on its own (the `CheckRules` walk visits untyped nodes too); `CheckRules::DeadVersionGuardArms` re-asks the same `VersionGuard.verdict` at diagnosis time and drops the diagnostics inside the dead arm, so the two readers cannot disagree about which arm is dead.
- `Prism::AndNode`/`Prism::OrNode` — after evaluating the LHS to obtain `left_scope`, call `Narrowing.predicate_scopes(node.left, left_scope)`. The RHS MUST be evaluated under the LHS's truthy scope for `&&` and the LHS's falsey scope for `||`. The post-scope MUST still be the join-with-nil-injection of `left_scope` and the RHS's post-scope (modelling "RHS sometimes ran") so half-bound names from the RHS continue to nil-inject. The result type MUST be `union(narrow_falsey(left_type), right_type)` for `&&` and `union(narrow_truthy(left_type), right_type)` for `||` (less the constant short-circuit in § Statement-Level Evaluation).
- **Value position (`AndNode` / `OrNode`).** `ExpressionTyper` MUST type an `&&` / `||` reached through `Scope#type_of` — an argument, a receiver, a literal element, or a write's value — by delegating to `StatementEvaluator#evaluate` and reading the value, exactly as the conditional rule above. The RHS narrowing, the constant short-circuit and its issue #313 optimistic-carrier decline then have one implementation, so the value of `x.finite? && x` MUST NOT depend on its position. Issue [#1016](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1016): a second typer typed the RHS in the entry scope (`Float | false` where the statement bound `false | finite-float`), carried the short-circuit the statement lacked, and resolved the #313 mark against the entry scope, so `(v = MAP[key]) || key` still discarded the fallback in value position.

### Boundaries

Slice 6 phase 1 + phase 2 binds local-variable narrowing for truthiness, `nil?`, class-membership predicates, and trusted equality/inequality predicates. The following surfaces are deliberately deferred to a follow-up:

- Equality narrowing is intentionally narrow: it does not promote user-defined equality, `eql?`, `===`, Float literals, broad `String`/`Symbol`/`Integer` domains, or `Dynamic[top]` into precise literal domains. Those comparisons may carry relational facts, but value narrowing waits for RBS/plugin-declared effects.
- Closure-captured locals are treated as ordinary locals at narrowing time. Slice 6 phase C sub-phase 3a introduces `Rigor::Inference::ClosureEscapeAnalyzer.classify(receiver_type:, method_name:, environment:, scope:)`, a pure query that returns `:non_escaping`, `:escaping`, or `:unknown` for a block-accepting call. Sub-phase 3a is RBS-blind: the analyzer ships a hardcoded catalogue covering core iteration methods (`Array`/`Hash`/`Range`/`Set`/`Enumerator`/`Enumerator::Lazy` Enumerable methods; `Hash#each_pair`/`each_key`/`each_value`/`transform_keys`/`transform_values`; `Range#step`; `Integer#times`/`upto`/`downto`; `IO`/`File`/`StringIO` line iteration — `each`, `each_line`, `each_byte`, `each_char`, `each_codepoint`, the singleton `IO.foreach`/`File.foreach` — plus the same Enumerable methods minus `chunk`/`chunk_while`/`slice_when`/`slice_before`/`slice_after`, which return an Enumerator that runs the block only when consumed (the collection entries still list those five, issue [#1311](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1311)); `Object#tap`/`then`/`yield_self`) as `:non_escaping` and a small set of known retainers (`Module#define_method`, `Class#define_method`, `Thread.new`/`start`/`fork`, `Fiber.new`, `Proc.new`) as `:escaping`. `Tuple` receivers project to `Array`, `HashShape` to `Hash`, and `Constant[v]` projects through the value's class. A `Nominal` receiver of a project class (`Scope#known_user_class?`) classifies through its ancestry when `scope:` is given (issue [#1234](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1234)): the method MUST be a catalogued iteration name that nothing in the project ancestry defines (`Scope#discovered_method_through_ancestors?`, `Scope#user_def_through_ancestors`), and the ancestor it resolves to MUST be catalogued — for a class the RBS environment knows, the `defined_in` of its instance-method definition; otherwise the first ancestor outside the project, in method-resolution order (`Scope#external_ancestor_name_candidates`), that declares the method. That ancestor is catalogued when it is a key of the class catalogue or `Enumerable`, whose entry is the stream entry's Enumerable methods minus `each`, which the includer supplies. An outside ancestor the RBS environment does not know may declare anything, so meeting one first MUST decline. So `class Shelf; include Enumerable; def each(&) = @items.each(&); end` classifies `Shelf#find` `:non_escaping`, and `Shelf#each`, or a `find` the class defines itself, stays `:unknown`. `Top`, `Dynamic[T]`, `Union`, and any other uncatalogued `(class, method)` pair return `:unknown`. The analyzer MUST NOT mutate scope or raise on unrecognised inputs; consumers MUST treat `:unknown` as conservatively as `:escaping` for fact retention. The analyzer is a pure query in sub-phase 3a.
- Slice 6 phase C sub-phase 3b wires `ClosureEscapeAnalyzer` into `StatementEvaluator#eval_call`. When a `Prism::CallNode` carries a `Prism::BlockNode` and the analyzer classifies the call as `:escaping` or `:unknown`, the evaluator MUST attach a `FactStore::Fact` to the post-call scope with `bucket: :dynamic_origin`, `predicate: :closure_escape`, `target: Target.new(kind: :closure, name: <method symbol>)`, `payload: { method_name:, classification: }`, and `stability: :unstable`. A `:non_escaping` classification (or any block-less call) MUST leave the post-call `Scope#fact_store` unchanged. Sub-phase 3c additionally drops the narrowed type of every captured outer local that the block body can rebind, replacing it with `Dynamic[top]` through `Scope#with_local` (which also invalidates the local's `local_binding` facts). The "captured outer local" set is computed by walking `BlockNode#body` for `Prism::LocalVariableWriteNode`s whose name (a) appears in `Scope#locals` of the call-site scope and (b) is NOT introduced by the block itself (block parameters from `BlockParameterBinder.bind`, plus the `;`-prefixed block-locals on `BlockParametersNode#locals`). Locals the block only reads MUST stay narrowed; writes to a name shadowed by a block parameter MUST NOT count, and neither may a write to a nested block's or lambda's own parameter or block-local of the same name: a write MUST NOT count when it resolves inside a block or lambda nested between the body and the write (its Prism `depth` is below that nesting count), the rule `CapturedLocals.content_mutations` applies to a mutation site's read, so the rebind set and the in-place set name the same `a`. Nor may a write inside a nested `def`, `class`, `module` or `class << self` body (or a `def`'s parameters), which opens a scope that sees no outer local; a `def (o = x).m` receiver, a `class Foo < (s = x)` superclass and a `class << (t = x)` target run in the enclosing scope and MUST still count. A write Prism resolves in the body's own scope is left to the block-introduced exclusion, since Prism puts an outer local there only when the parse never saw it declared, which a synthetic call-site scope can still bind. `a = [1]; [1, 2].map { |k| [[]].each { |a| a = k }; a.first }` therefore folds to `[1, 1]`, and `z = 0; [1, 2].map { |k| def helper; z = 5; end; z }` to `[0, 0]`, never the unmoved-pin floor of a rebind that never happened. The conservative drop replaces the type with `Dynamic[top]` and applies to the `:escaping` / `:unknown` path only. [ADR-56](../adr/56-block-captured-local-mutation.md) supersedes the drop for the `:non_escaping` path: `StatementEvaluator#eval_call` instead writes the block's captured-local rebindings back through a capped body-fixpoint join (`write_back_block_captures` / `content_writeback_block_captures`, sharing the `Inference::BodyFixpoint` helper with the loop-body fixpoint), so a non-escaping `each` / `times` / `upto` loop that rebinds or content-mutates an outer local keeps the joined post-state rather than the pre-call binding. The instance variables the body rebinds belong to the same set on both paths, as they do in the #587 (b) per-element fold below: `Inference::CapturedLocals.writes` with `ivars: true` also collects every ivar-write form (plain, operator, `||=`, `&&=`, and a multi-assign target), at any depth, whose name `Scope#ivar` binds in the call-site scope. A block shares its caller's `self`, so an ivar it rebinds outlives the call exactly as a captured local does, and `@n = 0; items.each { @n += 1 }; @n == 0` MUST NOT fold. An ivar still on its ADR-58 declaration-sourced binding MUST be left out on both paths: that seed is the union of every write in the class, this body's included, so nothing the body stores can move it, and rebinding it would drop the mark that keeps its nil from being diagnostic fuel — `node = @tail; node.value; @tail = Node.new(v)` in an `each` body would report a possible nil receiver the `while` spelling of the same loop does not. The drop MUST bind each remaining ivar to `Dynamic[top]` through `Scope#with_ivar`. The write-back MUST seed each one from its pre-call `Scope#ivar` binding and read and bind every name through the accessor of its own kind (`CapturedLocals.bound_type`, and `CapturedLocals.bind` for an ivar, which keeps its issue #286 optimistic mark), so `@t` cannot be confused with a local `t`. A block that rebinds both kinds MUST NOT answer them from one `BodyFixpoint` over both: its final pass widens every name while any name still moves, so an ivar counter beside `mode = :b` would turn `mode`'s converged `:a | :b` into `Symbol`, and a local counter beside `@mode = :b` would do the same to `@mode`. Each kind MUST first converge on its own, with the other at its pre-call binding — for the locals, exactly the fixpoint a block that rebinds no ivar gets. A name converged that way is wrong when it reads the other kind (`last = @n; @n += 1`, `@last = count`), so one more pass under the settled bindings MUST check every name, and a name whose exit leaves its settled binding MUST take its answer from one joint `BodyFixpoint` over both kinds. The check MUST repeat until no name moves, because a name read off a moved one (`first = last`) may move in turn. The body's last evaluation is therefore a pass under the settled bindings, and the scopes recorded inside the block MUST read those, not a pass that pinned one kind to its pre-call value. Two names of ONE kind still share their fixpoint, so an ivar beside an ivar counter widens on the final pass exactly as a local beside a local counter does. Locals keep the plain `Scope#with_local` binding on every one of these transitions. An ivar the block only reads MUST keep its exact binding, and a block that rebinds no local and no rebindable ivar MUST leave the post-call scope byte-identical. The content write-back and its escaping floor are still local-only: an ivar a block mutates in place (`@out << x`) keeps its pre-call element types ([#1208](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1208)).
- Issue #1095 — `Rigor::Inference::BlockCallTiming::EXACTLY_ONCE_IMMEDIATE` is the built-in block call-timing table for callees that yield immediately, exactly once, before returning: `Kernel#tap`, `Kernel#then`, `Kernel#yield_self`, keyed by declaring owner so a later `RBS::Extended` call-timing effect can absorb it alongside `ClosureEscapeAnalyzer`'s catalogue. `BlockCallTiming.exactly_once_call?(receiver_type:, method_name:, scope:)` answers true only when every receiver member resolves the name to a `Kernel` declaration: a project `def` on the class or a project ancestor (`Scope#discovered_method_through_ancestors?`), a top-level `def`, a project patch on `Object` / `Kernel` / `BasicObject` or on any RBS ancestor of the receiver's class (plus `Module` / `Class` for a singleton receiver), a signature whose `defined_in` is not `Kernel`, a carrier naming no class, or a project class reaching an ancestor no signature describes all answer false; a singleton receiver is answered only through its RBS singleton definition. `ExpressionTyper#call_dispatch_type_for` consults it after the #853 break-arm collection: for a `Prism::BlockNode` block with a body, no arguments, a catalogued call, a syntactic proof that the body cannot complete normally (`BlockCallTiming.never_completes_normally?`: every path ends in a block-level `break` / `return` / `redo` / `retry` or a Kernel-spelled `raise` / `fail` / `throw` / `exit` / `exit!` / `abort` that no project `def`, discovered method on any class or module, or `pre_eval:` patch redefines), AND a block-return pass (`block_return_type_for`) answering exactly `bot` — the pass alone is insufficient because a declared `-> bot` (a project signature, or `Kernel#loop` before #1107) makes it answer `bot` for a body that completes — the call's type is the union of the break arms alone (`bot` when none) instead of the dispatch result unioned with them. The block body is re-typed for that check, so the pre-gates run name-first.
- Issue #1107 — `ExpressionTyper#call_dispatch_type_for` passes the tier result through `loop_completion_type` BEFORE the #853 break-arm union: when the result is exactly `bot` and `BlockCallTiming.loop_may_complete?(call_node)` holds, the normal-return half becomes `Dynamic[top]` (recorded as `DynamicOrigin::EXPLICIT_UNTYPED`, `StopIteration#result` being declared `untyped`). `loop_may_complete?` requires the name `loop`, a receiver-less / `self.` / `Kernel.` / `::Kernel.` spelling, and a block: a block-pass and a `Prism::BlockNode` declaring any parameters always qualify, and any other `Prism::BlockNode` qualifies unless its body is empty or every node in it is in `STOP_ITERATION_FREE_NODES` (literals, local / ivar reads and writes, `self`, splat-free array literals, `if` / `unless` / `else` / `&&` / `||`, parentheses, `break` / `next` / `redo` / `return` and their arguments — nothing that dispatches a method). Normative in [control-flow-analysis.md](../type-specification/control-flow-analysis.md) § "`Kernel#loop` completes on `StopIteration`".
- Issue #587 (a) — the block-return threading gate (`ExpressionTyper#threaded_block_body_type`, which re-types a multi-statement block body through `StatementEvaluator` only when the tail reads a name the prefix changes) MUST count an in-place mutation in the prefix as a change, not only a variable write. A name counts when, at any depth of the prefix, the receiver of a `MutationWidening::SHAPE_MUTATORS` call or of a store through `[]=` that is not a call — `Prism::IndexOperatorWriteNode`, `IndexOrWriteNode`, `IndexAndWriteNode` and the `IndexTargetNode` a multi-assign, `rescue =>` or `for` writes through, listed once as `Inference::IndexWriteWidening::CONTENT_WRITE_NODE_CLASSES` — can evaluate to it (`Inference::ReceiverAlias.mutated_reads`: the locals, instance variables and `it` parameter `ReceiverAlias.candidates` can select, or the class variable or global the receiver reads or writes directly, parentheses aside — `(@@c ||= []) << x` mutates `@@c`). Both node sets and that receiver answer are the widening's own, read by the scan, by the straight-line widening (`Inference::MutationWidening.widen_receiver_aliases` and `joinable_receiver?`, which widen a local, instance variable, class variable, global or `it` receiver alike) and by the tail-only re-answer under suppression (`ExpressionTyper#prefix_answered_scope`, which files its mutation sites through it), so the three agree on the variable a receiver names when it reads one directly or selects among locals and instance variables. Two receiver forms stay outside that answer: a class variable or global reached only through a branch that selects it (`(f ? $a : $b) << x` widens neither, on any path), and an element read (`a[0] << x`), which the scan does not count — the straight-line `Inference::ElementReadWidening` and `CapturedLocals.content_mutations` widen the local such a read is rooted at, but not a global or class variable (`$a[0] << x`). Every read is named through `ReceiverAlias.read_name`, because `Prism::ItLocalVariableReadNode` carries no `name`: an `it` read names the local `:it`, both as a mutated receiver and as a tail read — except an `it` receiver under a block or lambda nested in the prefix, which is that closure's own parameter and names nothing (`[[]].each { it << w }; it` leaves the body's `it` as it was). Without the global and class-variable half, `$g = +"k"; r = m.synchronize { $g << v.to_s; $g }` typed `r` as `"k"` and `r == "k"` folded always-truthy on a program whose `r` is `"k1"` — and the straight-line widening had the same blind spot, so threading alone would not have moved it; without the `it` half, `[[], []].map { it << 1; it }` typed `[[], []]` where the `|a|` spelling widened. The ADR-56 block write-back never widens an `it` read into the enclosing scope, since `it` is always the innermost block's own parameter. `StatementEvaluator` widens all four in straight-line code — the three compound writes where they stand, an `IndexTargetNode` at the multi-assign, `for` index or rescue reference that owns it (§ Statement-Level Evaluation) — and inside a nested block (the ADR-56 captured-local write-back). Without the index-write half, `h = { a: 0 }; v = m.synchronize do h[:a] += 1; h[:a] end` typed `v` as the literal's `0`, and `v == 0` folded always-truthy on a program whose `v` is `1`. The per-element arity-cap floor (issue #617 below) MUST read the same predicate.
- Issue #587 (b) — the per-element Tuple fold (`ExpressionTyper#try_per_element_block_fold`: the `map` / `select` / `find` family over a `Tuple` receiver) types every position from one entry scope, which is a first-iteration pin for any captured outer local or instance variable the body rebinds. The fold MUST therefore compute the same captured-rebind name set as the write-back above (`Inference::CapturedLocals.writes`, shared with `write_back_block_captures` and the sub-phase 3c drop so the three cannot disagree) and add the instance variables, class variables and globals the body writes on the same terms (`Inference::CapturedLocals.writes` with `non_locals: true`: every write form of each kind, at any depth, bound in the call-site scope — none is captured, since the block shares the caller's `self`, its class and the process, but each outlives an iteration exactly as a captured local does). An attribute setter called on `self` — `self.w = v`, its `+=` / `||=` / `&&=` forms and a multi-assign `self.w` target — MUST count as a rebind of the instance variable it is named after (`@w`): the `attr_writer` / `attr_accessor` convention stores there and no write node shows it; a hand-written setter storing elsewhere is not seen. An ivar still on its ADR-58 declaration-sourced binding MUST be left out: that seed is the union of every write in the class, this body's included, so it holds no first-iteration pin, and binding it would only let the unmoved-pin floor below take a literal union the class really stores. Names keep their sigil, so the one name set cannot confuse `@t` with a local `t`. The fold MUST run the same `BodyFixpoint` over that set BEFORE typing any position (seeded from the call-site binding, the block parameter bound to the union of the receiver's elements, cap 3, final `Constant→Nominal` widen, `Dynamic[top]` floor on non-convergence), and bind each such name to its converged type in every position's entry scope through its own kind of binding (`Scope#with_local` / `#with_ivar`), under the parameter bindings so a parameter still shadows. The rebinding MUST keep the name's issue #286 optimistic nil-freeness mark, which a fresh write drops: the converged type stands for the binding across iterations, and without the mark `v.nil?` folds to `false` over a value that is nil-free only optimistically. It MUST also carry a mark any fixpoint pass's exit binding holds for the name, as `Scope#join` unions the marks of the scopes it joins, and each later pass MUST enter with the marks collected so far: `v = 5; [1, 2].map { |e| out = v.nil? ? false : true; v = xs.first; out }` enters nil-free for real and leaves optimistically, so without the exit mark the second position folded `v.nil?` to `false` and typed `[true, true]` for a runtime `[true, false]`. Class variables and globals carry no mark. Names the body does not rebind, block parameters and block-locals MUST keep their exact per-position binding — a predicate that decides on the element alone still folds. When the fold runs under block-body threading suppression (nested inside another threaded body) it MUST bind those names to `Dynamic[top]` instead of running the fixpoint, and a fixpoint failure MUST take the same floor, never the seed. A converged binding that is IDENTICAL to a value-pinned seed MUST take the floor too (issue #617): the fixpoint reads each pass's exit binding out of `StatementEvaluator`, which does not thread a write from every position. A rebind in one it types as a pure value (a `when` condition, a `yield` argument, and a call's operands until [#1223](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/1223) — `(seen += 1) == 2` as the whole body) leaves the exit scope on the entry binding, and the fixpoint converges on the pin it exists to remove. A `Tuple` or `HashShape` seed, alone or as a `Union` member, MUST count as value-pinned whatever its elements are: its arity and key set are one assignment's, and the capped pass's `Constant→Nominal` widen erases a value but never a shape. The fixpoint stops after three passes, so a rebind a counter guards past the third iteration runs in none of them: `count = 0; row = []; sizes = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6].map { |v| prev = row; count += 1; row = [v, v] if count > 3; prev.size }` converged `row` on its `[]` seed, typed `sizes` as six `0`s for a runtime `[0, 0, 0, 0, 2, 2]`, and fired `flow.always-truthy-condition` on `sizes.last == 2`; the same shape over `m = 0` widens to `Integer` on the capped pass, which covers a later rebind of the same class but not one that changes it (`m = nil if n == 5` still types every position `Integer`; issue #1260). For a shape the unmoved test only asks whether the seed already covers the converged type, so its cost is wider than a `Constant`'s: besides a threaded write restoring the entry value (`buf = []`) and a seed only a `next` arm rebinds, any rebind the seed covers is floored too — another value of the same type (`qr = n.divmod(3)` rebound to `n.divmod(i + 2)`), or `g ||= [0]` over a `[0]?` seed — and a method whose return value is such a fold is `sig.skipped.untyped-return` under `sig-gen`. A seed carrying no value or shape pinning MUST be left alone — nothing in it is one iteration's. The test MUST read the seed as a whole: a `0 | Integer` seed is floored too, although a threaded `x += 1` only joins back into it, because an unthreaded write storing another class (`log(x = nil)`) converges on the same seed and `x.nil?` would fold to `false`. Nor may a pass whose exit binding moved exempt a name: a narrowing (`next false unless x`) or a threaded prefix (`x ||= 0`) moves it while the rebind itself stays unthreaded. That is why the unmoved-pin test alone is not enough, and the fold MUST first scan the body for a rebind of a name that the pass's exit scope cannot see (`Inference::UnthreadedRebinds`): `seen = nil; [1, 2].find { |e| seen ||= 0; (seen += 1) == 2 }` threads the `||=`, the fixpoint left the `nil` seed, and every predicate read `0 + 1 == 2`. The scan MUST be a whitelist of the positions `StatementEvaluator` threads a write from, and MUST treat every other position as unthreaded, so a position the evaluator gains later floors a name needlessly rather than letting a pin through. The threaded positions are a statement; the body of `(…)`, `begin`, `rescue`, `else` and `ensure`; an assignment's value — every variable-write form, and an index `||=` / `&&=` / `op=`'s value but not its receiver or index arguments; an `if` / `unless` / `case` predicate and every branch, but not a `when` condition or an `in` guard; both operands of `&&` / `||`; a `while` / `until` predicate and body; a `for` index, collection and body; a multi-assign value and its local and instance-variable targets; the LOCAL a `rescue => e` reference, a `for` index, a pattern capture or `=~`'s named captures binds — the evaluator binds no class variable or global through any of these, and no instance variable outside a multi-assign (`a, $g = 1, :z` leaves `$g` where it was); and a nested block or lambda body, for a local or an instance variable — a call's write-back fixpoint, which joins the block's `next` and `break` paths, or its escaping-block floor covers both, and nothing covers a class variable or global there. Every other position is unthreaded: a call's receiver and arguments (so `self.w =` always is), an array or hash literal, an interpolation, a `return` / `next` / `break` value, a `rescue` modifier, a `def` or class body. The evaluator has threaded a call's operands, the literals, the interpolations and a `rescue` modifier since #1223 (the `Prism::CallNode` entry above), but the scan does not admit them yet, so a rebind there still takes the floor. A threaded rebind MUST count as unthreaded too when it textually precedes a jump whose path the exit binding misses — a `redo` or `retry` in the fold's body or a nested block, and a `while` / `until` / `for` loop's `next`, `break`, `redo` or `retry` — since no join sees the scope such a jump leaves with (a pass's exit and a nested block's write-back join every block-level `next`, and the write-back every `break`, through `StatementEvaluator#evaluate_invocation`, but a loop's continuation joins none of its `next` paths and not every `break` path), the rebind it made and the narrowing its guard applied both. The body runs forward-only, so a rebind after every such jump is not on a jump's path, and a jump that is its construct's final statement leaves with the exit scope itself and MUST NOT count. A rebind in an `ensure` runs after any jump in its `begin`, so under any such jump it MUST count. A rebind under a block's parameter or block-local of the same name is not a rebind of the outer name. A name the scan flags MUST take the `Dynamic[top]` floor whatever the fixpoint converged to, MUST be left out of the fixpoint, and MUST be bound to the floor under every pass, so a name that reads it converges over the floor rather than over the pin. Every name the scan does not flag MUST keep the fixpoint and every rule of this entry, the unmoved-pin floor included: it stays as the backstop for a path the scan's position list does not anticipate, so a `0 | Integer` seed whose rebinds are all threaded is still floored. The per-element threading arity cap does not floor a name this binding covers, ivars included: the fixpoint's cost is independent of the arity. `total = 0; [1, 2].map do total += 1; total end` therefore types `[Integer, Integer]`, never `[1, 1]`, and so do `@t = 0; [1, 2].map { @t += 1 }` and `$g = 0; [1, 2].map { $g += 1 }`; `seen = nil; [1, 2].find { |e| seen ||= 0; (seen += 1) == 2 }` answers `1 | 2 | nil`, never `nil`; and `s = nil; [1, 2].map { |e| s ||= 0; i = 0; while i < 1; i += 1; if i == 1; s += 1; next; end; end; s }` answers `[0 | Dynamic[top], 0 | Dynamic[top]]`, never `[0, 0]`. The same pin has a CONTENT half the rebind set cannot see: a captured outer local the body mutates IN PLACE is never rebound, so every position read it at its entry contents. An instance variable, class variable or global the body mutates in place MUST take the same binding, admitted on the rebind set's terms (`CapturedLocals.content_mutations` with `non_locals: true`; a class variable or global counts only as the mutated receiver itself, parenthesised or not, not through a branch selecting it — `$seen << x` after `n = $seen.size` folded `find` to `nil` otherwise): once the #587 (a) gate threads an index write, `@cache[:first] ||= e; @cache[:first] == 2` otherwise stores each position's own `e` into the empty entry hash and folds `find` to `2` where Ruby answers `nil`. The fold MUST collect those names on the same terms as the rebind set (`Inference::CapturedLocals.content_mutations`: a call to a `MutationWidening::SHAPE_MUTATORS` name, or an `IndexOrWriteNode` / `IndexAndWriteNode` / `IndexOperatorWriteNode` / `IndexTargetNode`, counted through every variable its receiver can evaluate to at any nesting depth as long as a local's read does not resolve inside a nested block (see the ADR-56 slice-A rule below), with block-introduced names and names the call-site scope does not bind excluded) and bind each, in every position's entry scope, to its call-site binding widened through every such site as the straight-line seam widens it for a store of UNKNOWN values (`Inference::UnknownStoreWidening.widen`: `MutationWidening.widen_for_mutator` fed one `Dynamic[top]` per argument, an index write storing through `[]=` with its index arguments ahead of the value). A local MUST also count through the two routes straight-line code widens it by without reading it as the mutated receiver, or every position reads it at its entry contents — `a = [[1]]; [1, 2].map { |e| v = a[0].size; a[0] << e; v }` and `a = [1]; [1, 2].map { |e| v = a.size; add_to(a, e); v }` both answered `[1, 1]` for a runtime `[1, 2]`. A site whose receiver is an element read rooted at the local (`a[0] << e`, `a.first.push(e)`; `ElementReadWidening.element_read_path`) is a site of that local, and its widening MUST run on the element the path selects and rebuild the carrier around it (`ElementReadWidening.widen_through_path`, with the unknown-store widening and gradual arm below applied to the element), so a sibling slot keeps its type and a path the straight-line seam cannot follow (a `HashShape` slot, `h[:a] << e`) declines as it does there. A self-call passing the local to a positional parameter its resolved user def content-mutates (`add_to(a, e)`; `StatementEvaluator#content_mutated_arguments`, the resolution and parameter scan of the ADR-57 callee floor) is an `UnknownStoreWidening::CalleeStore` site of it. A mutator name called on `self` (`self.store(h, k)`, `self.push(a, x)`) names no variable as its receiver, so it MUST be asked as such a self-call rather than taken for an in-place site on `self`. A callee store's widening MUST floor every `Array` / `Hash` / `String` carrier the binding can hold to its bare carrier (`Array[Dynamic[top]]`, `Hash[Dynamic[top], Dynamic[top]]`, `String`): a literal, a nominal (a precise one included, as the callee floor includes it), and a difference, refinement or intersection over one (`non-empty-array[Integer]`, `non-empty-string`, `decimal-int-string`, `non-empty-uppercase-string`), member by member through a `Union` so that a member no callee can fill (`nil`) stays (`UnknownStoreWidening.content_floor`). The straight-line callee and escaping-block floor (`StatementEvaluator#content_floor_for`) MUST likewise floor a String in any refined form to `String`, since the callee can empty or rewrite it: `reset(s)` over `s = RUBY_VERSION.upcase` otherwise left `s.empty?` at `false`. Both routes reach every consumer of `CapturedLocals.content_mutations`: the HashShape per-pair fold, the generic block-return pass and the ADR-56 slice-A pass entry below. The stored values MUST NOT be typed from the call's entry scope, since a value computed from the receiver's entry contents (`h[k] = h[k] + 1`) is itself a first-iteration answer. After each site's widening, every site that can store a value — anything but a mutator that only removes or reorders (`pop`, `shift`, `delete`, `delete_at`, `delete_if`, `reject!`, `select!`, `filter!`, `keep_if`, `clear`, `compact!`, `uniq!`, `slice!`, `sort!`, `sort_by!`, `reverse!`, `rotate!`, `shuffle!`), unless that site is the one that closes a literal `Tuple` / `HashShape` (alone or as a `Union` member) into a nominal — MUST join `Dynamic[top]` into every type argument of the resulting `Array` / `Hash` carrier (member-wise through a `Union`, through a `Difference`'s base): the widening joins argument evidence only for the plain adders, so a site like `map!`, a block-form `fill`, `transform_values!`, `merge!` or `flatten!` otherwise keeps the seed's element types, and `a = [1]; [1, 2].map { a.map!(&:to_s); a.first }` would answer `Integer` for a `"1"`. A site whose widening declines leaves the binding unchanged, as the straight-line seam does (a precise nominal, which is a claim the seam may not grow; or a name the carrier's mutator table does not list, such as `store` on a literal `Tuple`). An empty-witness `Array` / `Hash` refinement under a storing mutator that keeps the witness (`non-empty-array[String]` under `map!`, `non-empty-hash[K, V]` under `transform_values!`) is not such a decline: the straight-line seam grows it (issue #936), and a mutator whose arguments describe nothing it stores takes the unknown-store arm there (the unknown-store entry below), so the site widens and keeps the witness like any other storing site. A `Union` holding such a member takes the arm whole, a precise member included, as it does when any other member widens; a precise nominal on its own still declines, and so does a refinement under an adder called with no argument (`xs.push`), which stores nothing. `xs = gets.to_s.split(","); if xs.any?; r = [1, 2].map { xs.map!(&:to_sym); xs.first }; end` therefore types `r` as `[Dynamic[top], Dynamic[top]]`, never the `[String, String]` whose `r.last.to_proc` drew `call.undefined-method`, and above the per-element threading arity cap `xs` counts as answered rather than floored. The closing-remover exception exists because the nominal cannot say a slot or key may be missing, and without the arm a later adder declines it: `{ a: 0 }` under a lone `h.delete(:a)` read `h[:b]` as `0`, and `s = [0]` under `p = s.pop; s.push(x)` stayed `Array[0]`, so the answer depended on the order the sites were written in; a name whose binding no site moved MUST NOT be bound at all, since its entry binding says nothing about later iterations. That binding evaluates no body, so it MUST apply under threading suppression too; the rebind fixpoint MUST run over it, and a name the body both rebinds and mutates MUST take the same widening over its converged type, whether or not the widening moved its call-site binding — the rebind can bring back a fresh literal the next iteration mutates. The unmoved-pin floor above MUST compare the converged type with the fixpoint's seed but ask whether the CALL-SITE binding is value-pinned (for every other name the two are the same binding): the in-place widening has already erased the pin from the seed, so `s = +"ab"; [1, 2].map { [s, (s << "c" if s.is_a?(String)), (s &&= s.to_sym).size].first }` would otherwise believe `String` for a local that holds a Symbol. This inherits the unmoved-pin trade-off: a name the body mutates and visibly rebinds to the widened class (`t << "c"; t = t.strip`) converges on its seed too and MUST be floored with the hidden case. A statement rebind of the name MUST NOT exempt it, because converging on the widened seed next to one does not show that the rebind was all there was: the capped pass runs no rebind a counter guards past the third iteration (`s = s.dup; n += 1; s = [e] if n > 3` would type `String` for a runtime `[5, "c"]`), and the scans see only write nodes in the body, so a lambda defined outside it (`close = -> { cur = nil }; … close.call`) or `binding.local_variable_set` rebinds the local where no pass looks. A captured local the body does not mutate MUST keep its exact binding. `h = { a: 0 }; [:a, :a].map { |k| h[k] = h[k] + 1 }` therefore types `[Dynamic[top], Dynamic[top]]`, never `[1, 1]`; `h = { a: 0 }; [:a, :a].map do |k| h[k] += 1; h[k] end` types `[Dynamic[top] | Integer, Dynamic[top] | Integer]`, never `[0, 0]`; and `h = { a: 0 }; [:a, :a].map { |k| h[k] }` still folds to `[0, 0]`.
- ADR-56 slice A — a rebind that reads a capture the body mutates in place. `StatementEvaluator#write_back_block_captures` re-evaluates the block body once per fixpoint pass with the rebound names bound to the running assumption, so a captured local the body mutates IN PLACE (`Inference::CapturedLocals.content_mutations`, the #587 (b) set) re-entered every pass at its pre-call contents, and a rebind read from it recorded the first iteration's answer. Every pass MUST bind each such local the body does not rebind, under the rebound bindings, to its call-site binding widened through its mutation sites for a store of UNKNOWN values (`Inference::UnknownStoreWidening.widen`); the stored values MUST NOT be typed from the entry scope. A name the body both rebinds and mutates MUST take the same widening over the pass's running assumption, as #587 (b) widens it over its converged type: the assumption carries the exits of the body's straight-line seam, which can close the collection without a gradual arm. For either binding, when the widened result is still an `Array` / `Hash` nominal (alone, as a `Union` member, or as a refinement's base) with a value-pinned type argument, the pass MUST join `Dynamic[top]` into every type argument of that result: `s = [0, 9]; s.pop` leaves the pinned `Array[0 | 9]` a later `push` declines, and under `if s.any?` a `pop` in the body drops `non-empty-array[0 | 9]` to that same nominal first, so `top = s.last; s.push(x)` would read the pins on every pass. A `bool` or a signature's literal union counts as pinned; the arm can only quiet a report. A block that mutates nothing captured MUST run exactly the passes it ran before; the binding evaluates no body. `content_mutations` MUST NOT count a receiver read that resolves inside a block nested between the body and the mutation site (`LocalVariableReadNode#depth` below that nesting), or inside a nested `def` or class body but not its receiver, superclass or `class << self` target (a read resolving in the body's own scope is left to the block-introduced exclusion, as for `CapturedLocals.writes`), so a nested block's own parameter sharing the outer name (`[[9]].each { |a| a << x }`) is no mutation of the outer local. `a = [0]; last = nil; [1, 2].each { |x| last = a.last; a << x }` therefore types `last` as `0 | Dynamic[top] | nil`, never `0?`; `stack ||= [0]; top = stack.pop; stack.push(x)` in the same position types `top` as `Dynamic[top] | Integer | nil`; and the same rebind over a collection the body does NOT mutate (`first = base.last; out << x`) keeps `0?`, so `first == 1` still folds always-falsey.
- Issue #631 — the ADR-56 content join (`Inference::ContentJoin.join_array_content` / `join_hash_content`, shared by the block seam, the loop seam and `each_with_object`) rederives ONE collection carrier, and the seam writes it over the whole binding. It MUST therefore PARTITION the pre-state. A member the join reads as a carrier of the target class is absorbed into the rederived carrier: a `Tuple` or a `Nominal["Array"]` on the Array side and a `HashShape` or a `Nominal["Hash"]` on the Hash side (with or without type args, so `Array.new`'s bare nominal is absorbed and still closes), a `Difference` through its base, a `Union` member-wise. Every other member — a whole-variable `Dynamic`, a foreign `Nominal`, a `Constant` — MUST survive whole beside the rederived carrier; it contributes no element / key / value evidence and MUST NOT be examined a second time, so nothing is double-widened. `out = flag ? u : [2]; [1].each { out << 2 }` therefore types `Dynamic[top] | Array[2]`, never `Array[2]`. The single exception is `nil` (`Constant[nil]` / `Nominal["NilClass"]`), which MUST NOT survive: no content mutator is defined on `NilClass`, so the arm is live only on the zero-iteration path the loop / block base scope already models, and a live nil receiver is reported at the mutation itself. A pre-state carrying both an Array and a Hash carrier member (`StatementEvaluator#content_kind`'s `:mixed`) MUST instead rederive TWO carriers (`ContentJoin.join_mixed_content`): the Array side absorbs the members `array_residue` absorbs and joins the body's element evidence, the Hash side joins the pair evidence over the rest, and the Hash side's residue survives beside both. The seam cannot tell which member a store reached, so `StatementEvaluator#mixed_content_evidence` routes an index store (`[]=` or an index write) by its index: one no Array accepts (every member of some index a `Symbol`, `String`, `nil` or boolean, where `[1][:k] = v` raises `TypeError`) is the Hash side's alone, and one that could reach either member MUST floor both sides to `Dynamic[top]`. Read precisely, its value lands on the side it never reached, and a hand-written `-> (Array[Integer] | Hash[Symbol, String])` rejects the `Array["t" | Integer]` a guarded `x[:b] = "t" if x.is_a?(Hash)` made of the Array member; the Hash side takes an Integer key `x[0] = 5` stored in the Array member the same way. Every other adder belongs to one class and is read as the single-class join reads it. Joined as one Hash carrier, the Array member was residue and kept the literal shape the seed had: `y = gets ? [1] : { a: 1 }; [0].each { y << 2 if y.is_a?(Array) }` read `[1]` and `y.size == 1` folded always-truthy. Both sides read the same seed from before `widen_after_block` (#586), so an empty `[]` member still contributes no gradual element: `e = gets ? [] : {}; [0].each { e << 1 if e.is_a?(Array); e[:k] = 2 if e.is_a?(Hash) }` types `Array[1] | Hash[:k, 2]`. Taking the residue from the widened scope instead would keep the arity-forget and lose the element: `Array[1]` still folds `y.last == 2` always-falsey.
- Issue #645 — the straight-line twin of the rule above. `Inference::MutationWidening.widen_for_mutator` MUST map a `Union` pre-state MEMBERWISE rather than declining it: every member the mutator's own class carries (a `Tuple` or an empty-witness `Difference` on the Array side, a `HashShape` or its `Difference` on the Hash side — the absorbed side of `ContentJoin`'s residue partition, asked per member so a selector in BOTH mutator tables answers for each carrier) widens exactly as the same member widens outside a union, and every other member MUST survive whole. The union is left untouched (no widening at all) when no member is such a carrier, or when every carrier is already nominal. `out = flag ? 5 : [2]; out << 2` therefore types `5 | Array[2 | Dynamic[top] | Integer]`, never `5 | [2]` — a retained arity the mutation falsified, which a later `out.size == 1` folds on.
- The [#540](https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/issues/540) mutation census — `ScopeIndexer#collect_literal_receiver_mutations` records the constants and class variables a call mutates by NAME, not by method, so `widen_mutated_constants` and its class-variable twin `widen_mutated_cvars` cannot apply the per-method widening of these rules and cannot know what the call stored. Each MUST wrap a mutated entry in `Dynamic[…]` and MUST first unpin every carrier member of it, alone or as a `Union` member, and of the facet of an entry that is already `Dynamic` (`ScopeIndexer#census_mutated_type`): a closed `HashShape` reopens to `extra_keys: :open` with its pairs and key policy unchanged, a `Tuple` becomes `Array[union(elements) | Dynamic[top]]`, an `Array` / `Hash` nominal with a value-pinned type argument (`UnknownStoreWidening.value_pinned_collection?`, the test the ADR-56 slice-A unknown-store binding applies) gains a `Dynamic[top]` arm on every type argument (`UnknownStoreWidening.gradual_content`), and a `Difference` / `Refined` member drops to its base, unpinned the same way, since the mutation can falsify the removal or the refinement too (`clear` empties a `non-empty-array`).
